## Acts 11:1–18 (id: 9304)

Jews traditionally kept themselves separate and did not eat or interact in a social setting with Gentiles ([10:28](https://ref.ly/Acts10:28); [22:21–22](https://ref.ly/Acts22:21-Acts22:22); see [John 4:9](https://ref.ly/John4:9), [27](https://ref.ly/John4:27); [18:28](https://ref.ly/John18:28); [Galatians 2:12–14](https://ref.ly/Gal2:12-Gal2:14)). The Jewish Christians in Jerusalem learned that Gentiles had received the word of God. Then they criticized Peter's unusual actions and asked for an explanation, which Peter provided.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:1-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Circumcision (ID: `keyterm:Circumcision`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); John (ID: `person:John`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Linen Cloth (ID: `realia:LinenCloth`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Creeping Animal (ID: `fauna:CreepingAnimal`)

## Acts 11:4–17 (id: 9315)

Peter reviewed what had happened. He explained that God had directed the whole series of events ([11:12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:12)). Peter ate with Gentiles (non\-Jews) because God made it evident that he should ([11:4–12](https://ref.ly/Acts11:4-Acts11:12)). Peter then saw the Holy Spirit come upon the Gentiles, realizing God accepted and blessed them like Jews who trusted in Jesus ([11:15–17](https://ref.ly/Acts11:15-Acts11:17); see [1:5](https://ref.ly/Acts1:5)). Peter followed God's will by admitting Gentiles to the church.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:4-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Joppa (ID: `place:Joppa`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); John (ID: `person:John`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Discern (ID: `keyterm:Discern`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Linen Cloth (ID: `realia:LinenCloth`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Creeping Animal (ID: `fauna:CreepingAnimal`)

## Acts 11:18 (id: 9305)

Peter gave a clear and careful explanation. Those who had objected were convinced. They saw that God was at work in the Gentiles' believing in Jesus and in their receiving eternal life.

However, the question of including Gentiles was not finished. Soon it would lead to a serious crisis in the church ([15:1–35](https://ref.ly/Acts15:1-Acts15:35); see also Paul's letter to the Galatians).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Acts 11:19–26 (id: 9306)

* After Stephen's death, persecution forced Christians to move to other areas ([8:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts8:1-Acts8:3)). They traveled as far as Phoenicia, Cyprus, and Antioch.
* Antioch in Syria was a busy, diverse city. It was the third\-largest city in the Roman Empire, after Rome and Alexandria. Antioch played a key role in spreading the Christian message to non\-Jewish people.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:19-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Cypriots (ID: `group:Cyprus`); Hellenists (ID: `group:Hellenist`); Phoenicia (ID: `place:Phoenicia`); Christians (ID: `group:Christ`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Tarsus (ID: `place:Tarsus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Stephen (ID: `person:Stephen`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Good (ID: `keyterm:Good`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Greece (ID: `place:Greece`)

## Acts 11:21–24 (id: 9308)

As with the household of Cornelius, many Gentiles believed in Jesus. The growth of Christian faith among Gentiles had to remain in harmony with the church at Jerusalem. So, the Jerusalem Christians sent Barnabas to Antioch to oversee the developments there. Barnabas could see that God’s blessing was on what was happening, so he supported it with joy.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:21-24

## Acts 11:28 (id: 9312)

Claudius was the nephew of Tiberius Caesar ([Luke 3:1](https://ref.ly/Luke3:1)). He ruled as Roman emperor from AD 41 to AD 54\.

His last wife was his niece Agrippina. Claudius adopted her son, Nero.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:28

## Acts 11:29–30 (id: 9313)

Agabus's prophecy resulted in the Christians in Antioch helping the Jewish Christians in Judea. They gave as much as they could and trusted the funds to Barnabas and Saul. This act of love and support showed the difference Jesus had made in their lives. Saul (also called Paul) mentions this visit in his letter to the Galatians ([Galatians 2:1–10](https://ref.ly/Gal2:1-Gal2:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`)

## Acts 11:30 (id: 9314)

This is the first mention in the book of Acts of elders as officers in the Christian church (see also [14:23](https://ref.ly/Acts14:23); [15:2–23](https://ref.ly/Acts15:2-Acts15:23); [16:4](https://ref.ly/Acts16:4); [20:17–35](https://ref.ly/Acts20:17-Acts20:35); [21:18](https://ref.ly/Acts21:18); compare [1 Timothy 3:1–7](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:1-1Tim3:7); [Titus 1:5–9](https://ref.ly/Titus1:5-Titus1:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 11:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`)

## Acts 12:1–5 (id: 9316)

* Jesus had prophesied that his followers would experience persecution and difficulty ([Luke 11:49–51](https://ref.ly/Luke11:49-Luke11:51)). For the first time since his death, Roman authorities acted violently against the church. James, the brother of John, was among the first disciples Jesus called ([Mark 1:16–20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:16-Mark1:20); [Luke 5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Luke5:1-Luke5:11)). He was one of the first Christians killed because of his faith.
* King Herod Agrippa took action to intentionally harm the church ([Acts 12:20–23](https://ref.ly/Acts12:20-Acts12:23)). It is possible that doing this was meant to strengthen his political alignment with the Jewish people. The letter of James, the brother of Jesus, was probably written after this persecution effort to the Christians who were scattered (see [8:1–4](https://ref.ly/Acts8:1-Acts8:4); James Book Introduction, “Date of Writing”).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); James (ID: `person:James`); Herod (ID: `person:Herod.3`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Acts 12:4 (id: 9321)

Herod had Peter guarded by four squads of soldiers, making it impossible for the apostle to escape (compare [12:6](https://ref.ly/Acts12:6)). God was in control, even though the circumstances seemed impossible for humans to overcome (compare [4:27–31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:27-Acts4:31); [Genesis 18:14](https://ref.ly/Gen18:14); [Jeremiah 32:17](https://ref.ly/Jer32:17), [27](https://ref.ly/Jer32:27); [Matthew 19:26](https://ref.ly/Matt19:26); [Mark 10:27](https://ref.ly/Mark10:27); [Luke 1:37](https://ref.ly/Luke1:37); [18:27](https://ref.ly/Luke18:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Acts 12:5 (id: 9322)

*the church was fervently praying*: God answers the earnest prayers of his people ([12:6–17](https://ref.ly/Acts12:6-Acts12:17); see [Luke 11:1–13](https://ref.ly/Luke11:1-Luke11:13); [18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Luke18:1-Luke18:8); compare [Matthew 7:7–11](https://ref.ly/Matt7:7-Matt7:11); [John 15:7](https://ref.ly/John15:7); [Philippians 4:6–7](https://ref.ly/Phil4:6-Phil4:7); [James 5:16](https://ref.ly/Jas5:16); [1 John 3:22](https://ref.ly/1John3:22)).

## Acts 12:6–19 (id: 9323)

God, through an angel, guided Peter out of prison. Peter reunited with his praying friends, and God sent him to continue proclaiming the good news about Jesus. The message advanced by God's power, even when the church and apostles faced strong opposition and discouragement.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:6-19

## Acts 12:18–23 (id: 9319)

When Peter was missing after a thorough search, Herod questioned the guards and executed them (see [16:27](https://ref.ly/Acts16:27)). However, Herod himself then experienced a painful death. God punished him for his pride when he accepted the people's worship. The ancient Jewish historian Josephus provides more details about the death of Herod Agrippa I (Josephus, *Antiquities* 19\.8\.1–2\).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 12:18-23

## Acts 13:1 (id: 9333)

* *prophets and teachers*: See “The Gift of Prophecy” Theme Note; also see [1 Corinthians 12:28–29](https://ref.ly/1Cor12:28-1Cor12:29); [Ephesians 4:11](https://ref.ly/Eph4:11).
* The name Simeon suggests a Jewish background (see [Genesis 29:33](https://ref.ly/Gen29:33); [Luke 2:25](https://ref.ly/Luke2:25); [3:30](https://ref.ly/Luke3:30)). He is also called Niger (which means "the black mane"). This suggests that he was probably African.
* Lucius is a Latin name. He came from Cyrene, the capital of Libya in North Africa. He was probably one of the preachers from Cyrene who brought the Christian message to Antioch ([Acts 11:20](https://ref.ly/Acts11:20)).
* Manaen grew up with King Herod Antipas. He was probably Luke’s source for understanding Antipas’s thoughts and actions (see [Luke 9:7–9](https://ref.ly/Luke9:7-Luke9:9)).
* Barnabas and Saul play important roles in the following story.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:1
* **Associated Articles:** The Gift of Prophecy (ID: `500390@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Manaen (ID: `person:Manaen`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.4`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`)

## Acts 13:1–3 (id: 9325)

*prophets and teachers:* these leaders of the church at Antioch spent a lot of time in worship and prayer. They had an intense desire to do the Lord's will, and they fasted to spiritually prepare themselves to receive guidance from God. As they prayed, the Holy Spirit spoke to them. 

The church set apart Barnabas and Saul, recognizing God's clear call for them to do a special work in his name. The inner journey these Christians took to pray and listen to God matched their outer journey of service. Their service included proclaiming the Christian faith and performing works of healing and salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Manaen (ID: `person:Manaen`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.4`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Cyrene (ID: `place:Cyrene`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Acts 13:3 (id: 9340)

*they laid their hands on them*: They performed this holy act only after more fasting and prayer*.* The Pastoral Letters (1 Timothy, 2 Timothy, and Titus) caution against appointing someone as a Christian leader without proper care and background checks ([1 Timothy 5:22](https://ref.ly/1Tim5:22)). At this point, the church at Antioch sent Barnabas and Saul as its representatives for the mission.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Acts 13:4 (id: 9345)

* Barnabas and Saul were commissioned by the church at Antioch to go on their first missionary journey. They were guided by the Holy Spirit.
* Seleucia was the ancient seaport city of Antioch, about 20 kilometers (12 miles) west of the city at the opening of the Orontes River.
* *from there to Cyprus:* Barnabas was from Cyprus, the next stop on their journey ([4:36](https://ref.ly/Acts4:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Seleucia (ID: `place:Seleucia`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`)

## Acts 13:5 (id: 9348)

When they arrived in Salamis on the eastern end of Cyprus, they visited the Jewish synagogues (see study note on 9:2). There, Jews could hear and respond to the Christian message. They would also meet people who had converted to Judaism (those who had chosen to follow Israel’s God; see study note on 13:43). They also met Gentiles (non\-Jews) who were seeking God. These Gentiles are sometimes called *God\-fearers* (people who worshiped the God of Israel but had not fully become Jews; see study note on 10:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:5
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 10:2 (ID: `9296@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Acts 13:43 (ID: `9343@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Acts 9:2 (ID: `9662@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salamis (ID: `place:Salamis`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Mark (ID: `person:Mark`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Acts 13:6 (id: 9350)

* *Paphos:* This major city was on the southwest coast of Cyprus, a location that had strategic significance.
* *a Jewish sorcerer and false prophet named Bar\-Jesus*: People from the east who practiced magic like this man often had significant power to influence people in the Greek and Roman world.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paphos (ID: `place:Paphos`); Bar-jesus (ID: `person:Bar-jesus`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Acts 13:6–12 (id: 9349)

In Paphos, a false prophet caused a power struggle. This resulted in a display of God's power, and the Roman proconsul (governor) became a Christian.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:6-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bar-jesus (ID: `person:Bar-jesus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Sergius Paulus (ID: `person:SergiusPaulus`); Paphos (ID: `place:Paphos`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`)

## Acts 13:7–8 (id: 9351)

Sergius Paulus, the proconsul (governor), was an intelligent person. He had interest in the teachings of Barnabas and Saul. But Elymas (the sorcerer’s Greco\-Roman name) recognized a challenge to his power and strongly opposed the message of Barnabas and Saul.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:7-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sergius Paulus (ID: `person:SergiusPaulus`); Bar-jesus (ID: `person:Bar-jesus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Acts 13:9 (id: 9352)

Luke marks an important shift in using the name Saul (a Hebrew name) and Paul (a Greco\-Roman name). This may show that Paul is now focusing on his mission to Gentiles (non\-Jews). For the rest of Acts, Luke calls him Paul, except when Paul tells the story of his conversion (as in [22:7](https://ref.ly/Acts22:7), [13](https://ref.ly/Acts22:13); [26:14](https://ref.ly/Acts26:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`)

## Acts 13:10–11 (id: 9326)

Paul, who had become the main speaker, confronted the sorcerer. He rebuked his false claims, exposed his deceit, and announced God’s judgment (see [8:20–24](https://ref.ly/Acts8:20-Acts8:24)). The sorcerer became blind immediately, and this condition lasted for a significant period of time. This event showed the power and truthfulness of the gospel message over the sorcerer's false claims.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`)

## Acts 13:12 (id: 9327)

The teaching about the Lord included a miracle showing divine power (compare [Mark 1:21–27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:27)). This was because it was a teaching about the living God (see [Acts 14:15](https://ref.ly/Acts14:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Acts 13:13–14 (id: 9328)

* They arrived at the port of Perga. From there, major roads led to the interior of the land beyond the Taurus Mountains.
* At this point, John Mark left the team for unknown reasons (see study note on 15:36–41). He might have been unhappy that the gospel was advancing into non\-Jewish lands. Perhaps he missed his home or was unable to continue the challenging journey. Whatever the reasons, he returned to the familiar and comfortable Jewish area of Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:13-14
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 15:36-41 (ID: `9375@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perga (ID: `place:Perga`); Pamphylia (ID: `place:Pamphylia`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch.2`); Paphos (ID: `place:Paphos`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Pisidia (ID: `place:Pisidia`); Mark (ID: `person:Mark`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Acts 13:14 (id: 9329)

Paul and Barnabas traveled inland to the high country and reached Pisidian Antioch. This Antioch was in the Roman province of Galatia in Asia Minor. This city is not the same one as the city of Antioch in Syria. 

As usual, the apostles began their work at the synagogue (see study note on 9:2; compare [13:5](https://ref.ly/Acts13:5); [14:1](https://ref.ly/Acts14:1); [17:1](https://ref.ly/Acts17:1), [2](https://ref.ly/Acts17:2), [10](https://ref.ly/Acts17:10), [17](https://ref.ly/Acts17:17); [18:4](https://ref.ly/Acts18:4), [19](https://ref.ly/Acts18:19); [19:8](https://ref.ly/Acts19:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:14
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 9:2 (ID: `9662@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch.2`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Pisidia (ID: `place:Pisidia`); Perga (ID: `place:Perga`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Acts 13:15 (id: 9330)

People chosen to read in the synagogue read the usual Scriptures for the day. This included one passage from the books of Moses and another from the prophets (see [Luke 4:16–28](https://ref.ly/Luke4:16-Luke4:28)). Then the synagogue leaders invited the visitors to share a word of encouragement for the people.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Law (ID: `keyterm:Law`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Acts 13:16–41 (id: 9331)

Paul accepted the invitation, and signaled for his audience to be quiet (compare [19:33](https://ref.ly/Acts19:33); [21:40](https://ref.ly/Acts21:40)). He began to clearly proclaim the good news about Jesus. This is Paul's first major speech in the book of Acts. It serves as a model of the way he preached to a Jewish audience (see [22:1–21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:1-Acts22:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:16-41

## Acts 13:17–22 (id: 9332)

To establish common ground with his audience, Paul traced Jewish history starting from the Israelite exodus from Egypt. He emphasized: 

* the Israelites' escape from Egyptian slavery,
* their settlement in Canaan,
* the establishment of the kingdom of Israel,
* the removal of King Saul, and
* the special role of King David.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:17-22

## Acts 13:22 (id: 9334)

Even though he sinned, David was a man who desired to completely follow God's ways (for example, [2 Samuel 11–12](https://ref.ly/2Sam11:1-2Sam12:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesse (ID: `person:Jesse`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Acts 13:23–25 (id: 9335)

At this point, Paul shifted to the main point of his message. Jesus, descended from King David's family line, was the Savior God promised to Israel. John the Baptist prepared the way for the Messiah (God's chosen leader). John called Israel to repent and be baptized. John was a humble servant who prepared the way for the one whose coming he announced.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:23-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); John (ID: `person:John`); Promise (ID: `keyterm:Promise`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Savior (ID: `keyterm:Savior`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`)

## Acts 13:26–37 (id: 9336)

Paul reviewed how Jesus had been treated with shame, including an unjust sentence and death. But God raised Jesus from the dead, and many witnesses saw him. This message is good news, because through Jesus people can receive forgiveness of sins. But people must respond with faith, or they will face serious consequences.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:26-37

## Acts 13:43 (id: 9343)

* Full converts to Judaism (also called *proselytes*) were Gentiles who had gone through the rite of circumcision to become full members of the Jewish community, observing the Jewish law (see also [2:11](https://ref.ly/Acts2:11); [6:5](https://ref.ly/Acts6:5); [Matthew 23:15](https://ref.ly/Matt23:15)).
* *the grace of God*: The grace of God is an important idea in the New Testament. It describes God’s kindness and favor that people do not earn, shown most clearly through Jesus Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Acts 13:44–49 (id: 9344)

People were excited by the preaching of the apostles. A large gathering formed the next week. This response made some Jews jealous (see [4:1–2](https://ref.ly/Acts4:1-Acts4:2); [5:17](https://ref.ly/Acts5:17)). This was because Paul's ministry was attracting more converts than Judaism ([13:43](https://ref.ly/Acts13:43)). These Jews made verbal attacks against Paul and his ministry (see [6:8–12](https://ref.ly/Acts6:8-Acts6:12); [18:6](https://ref.ly/Acts18:6); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Acts19:9); [Matthew 23:13](https://ref.ly/Matt23:13)). 

Paul responded to the attacks with a bold statement that the jealous Jews had rejected their past opportunities to hear the word of God. Paul would now proclaim the message of salvation to Gentiles, following the Lord's command in Scripture (see [Acts 10:34–35](https://ref.ly/Acts10:34-Acts10:35)). The local Gentiles welcomed the gospel, and many accepted it. They spread the Lord's message throughout the region.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:44-49

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Resentment (ID: `keyterm:Resentment`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Acts 13:50–51 (id: 9346)

Jewish opposition once again forced Paul and Barnabas out of their district. They shook the dust off their feet as a sign of rejection, as Jesus had taught his disciples (see [Matthew 10:14–15](https://ref.ly/Matt10:14-Matt10:15); [Mark 6:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark6:11-Mark6:12); [Luke 9:5–6](https://ref.ly/Luke9:5-Luke9:6); [10:10–11](https://ref.ly/Luke10:10-Luke10:11)). That place was then treated as pagan (not worshiping the true God). Paul and Barnabas left that place in order to reach other people who might be more open to the message of new life in Christ.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:50-51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Iconium (ID: `place:Iconium`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Acts 13:52 (id: 9347)

Even though they probably encountered harassment and persecution, the disciples *were filled with joy and with the Holy Spirit* (compare [5:41](https://ref.ly/Acts5:41); [16:23–25](https://ref.ly/Acts16:23-Acts16:25); [Matthew 5:10–12](https://ref.ly/Matt5:10-Matt5:12); [2 Corinthians 8:2](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:2); [1 Thessalonians 1:6](https://ref.ly/1Thess1:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 13:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`)

## Acts 14:1 (id: 9356)

* Paul and Barnabas traveled on their mission to the next town, Iconium (now called Konya). This city is east of Pisidian Antioch on a high plateau in south\-central Asia Minor. Iconium was near several important trade routes.
* In their usual way, Paul and Barnabas began their ministry by speaking powerfully in the Jewish synagogue. Their teachings led to a positive response from both Jews and Greeks.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Iconium (ID: `place:Iconium`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Acts 14:2 (id: 9360)

Once again, Paul and Barnabas faced Jews who opposed them. These people rejected the Christian message (the good news about Jesus) and also influenced the Gentiles to think wrongly about it.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Acts 14:3 (id: 9361)

The Spirit\-inspired apostles were resilient to withstand the verbal attack against them, and they persisted in preaching the Good News that God’s grace was available to Gentiles as well as to Jews. • boldly: Spirit\-inspired boldness is evident throughout Acts (see [2:14](https://ref.ly/Acts2:14); [4:9–10](https://ref.ly/Acts4:9-Acts4:10), [13](https://ref.ly/Acts4:13); [7:2–53](https://ref.ly/Acts7:2-Acts7:53); [8:30–35](https://ref.ly/Acts8:30-Acts8:35); [9:27–28](https://ref.ly/Acts9:27-Acts9:28); [18:26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26); [19:8](https://ref.ly/Acts19:8); [22:3–21](https://ref.ly/Acts22:3-Acts22:21); [23:1–6](https://ref.ly/Acts23:1-Acts23:6); [28:16–20](https://ref.ly/Acts28:16-Acts28:20), [23–31](https://ref.ly/Acts28:23-Acts28:31)). • In addition, the Holy Spirit confirmed the Christian message with miraculous signs and wonders (cp. [5:12–16](https://ref.ly/Acts5:12-Acts5:16); [15:12](https://ref.ly/Acts15:12); [16:18](https://ref.ly/Acts16:18); [19:11](https://ref.ly/Acts19:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Portent (ID: `keyterm:Portent`)

## Acts 14:4 (id: 9362)

The apostolic message required a decision about belief in Jesus, and opinion was clearly split. • Paul and Barnabas are called apostles for the first time (also in [14:14](https://ref.ly/Acts14:14)), extending the idea of apostleship beyond the Twelve. Their message was in continuity with that of the original apostles, and they were prepared to suffer hardship and persecution for it as the earlier apostles had done ([5:41](https://ref.ly/Acts5:41); [14:19](https://ref.ly/Acts14:19); [20:24](https://ref.ly/Acts20:24); [21:13–14](https://ref.ly/Acts21:13-Acts21:14); see also [Rom 8:35–38](https://ref.ly/Rom8:35-Rom8:38); [2 Cor 4:8–17](https://ref.ly/2Cor4:8-2Cor4:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jews (ID: `group:Jews`)

## Acts 14:6 (id: 9363)

Lycaonia was a southern region of the Roman province of Galatia. Its major cities were Lystra, Derbe, Laranda, and Iconium. Acts reports two more visits by Paul to Lycaonia ([16:1–5](https://ref.ly/Acts16:1-Acts16:5); [18:23](https://ref.ly/Acts18:23)). Paul’s letter to the Galatians was probably addressed to scattered believers in the churches of Lycaonia (see Galatians Book Introduction, “Recipients”). • Lystra was located roughly twenty\-five miles (40 km) south\-southwest of Iconium. • Derbe*,* situated about thirty miles (50 km) southeast of Lystra, was on the busy major road that stretched from Iconium and Lystra eastward to Tarsus, the capital of Cilicia.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lycaonia (ID: `place:Lycaonia`); Derbe (ID: `place:Derbe`); Lystra (ID: `place:Lystra`)

## Acts 14:8–20 (id: 9364)

Paul’s healing of a man with crippled feet recalls a similar incident in [3:1–12](https://ref.ly/Acts3:1-Acts3:12). In Acts, the work of Paul parallels the work of Peter, and the many miraculous signs and wonders performed among the Jews were also performed among the Gentiles.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:8-20

## Acts 14:9 (id: 9365)

Paul realized he had faith to be healed: Paul, full of the Holy Spirit ([9:17](https://ref.ly/Acts9:17)), sensed the man’s spiritual openness and expectancy for divine help and intervention.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Acts 14:11–13 (id: 9353)

Zeus was the patron god of Lystra, and the city had a temple in his honor. They associated Barnabas with Zeus, perhaps because he was the more impressive figure, and Hermes was identified with Paul . . . since he was the chief speaker. The native people of Lystra thought that they were being treated to a divine visitation similar to a past mythological appearance cited by the poet Ovid (in which the gods Zeus and Hermes came to visit the area but were unrecognized except by an old couple; see Ovid, *Metamorphoses* 8\.616–724\). So the people set out to honor these supposed gods.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 14:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zeus (ID: `deity:Zeus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Hermes (ID: `deity:Hermes`); Lycaonians (ID: `group:Lycaonia`); Garland (ID: `realia:Garland`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Barnabas (ID: `person:Barnabas`); Lycaonia (ID: `place:Lycaonia`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Acts 14:26–28 (id: 9359)

As soon as Paul and Barnabas returned home to Antioch from their first missionary journey, they called the church together and gave a full report of their evangelism and discipleship. They humbly acknowledged divine guidance in opening the door of faith to the Gentiles (see [11:18](https://ref.ly/Acts11:18); [1 Cor 16:9](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:9); [2 Cor 2:12](https://ref.ly/2Cor2:12)). Similar reports ([Acts 15:4](https://ref.ly/Acts15:4), [12](https://ref.ly/Acts15:12); [21:19](https://ref.ly/Acts21:19)) always stressed the activity of God as working through the ministries of his servants (see [Rom 15:17–18](https://ref.ly/Rom15:17-Rom15:18); [1 Cor 3:5–9](https://ref.ly/1Cor3:5-1Cor3:9); [15:10–11](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:10-1Cor15:11)).

## Acts 15:1 (id: 9369)

These men from Judea were Jewish Christians who taught the necessity of circumcision (and with it, conversion to Judaism) in order to be saved. The central issue was how Gentiles were to be received into Christian fellowship. Paul wrote his letter to the Galatians about this time to counter the same teaching in Galatia (see Galatians Book Introduction, “Date of Writing”).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:1

## Acts 15:20 (id: 9371)

Eating food offered to idols is sinful if it involves knowingly partaking of an idolatrous sacrifice (see [Exod 20:4](https://ref.ly/Exod20:4); [Deut 5:8](https://ref.ly/Deut5:8); [1 Cor 8:4–13](https://ref.ly/1Cor8:4-1Cor8:13); [10:14–30](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:14-1Cor10:30); [Rev 2:14](https://ref.ly/Rev2:14), [20](https://ref.ly/Rev2:20)). • Sexual immorality was common in the Greek world but is always sinful ([Exod 20:14](https://ref.ly/Exod20:14); [Deut 5:18](https://ref.ly/Deut5:18); [Gal 5:19](https://ref.ly/Gal5:19)). • eating the meat of strangled animals: God’s law prohibits eating meat that has blood in it ([Lev 17:13–14](https://ref.ly/Lev17:13-Lev17:14)) or consuming blood ([Lev 17:10–12](https://ref.ly/Lev17:10-Lev17:12)), “for the life of every creature is in its blood” ([Lev 17:14](https://ref.ly/Lev17:14)). Also, “It is the blood, given in exchange for a life, that makes purification possible” (see [Lev 17:11](https://ref.ly/Lev17:11) and study note). This command was first given to Noah, the ancestor of Gentiles as well as Jews ([Gen 9:4](https://ref.ly/Gen9:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:20
* **Associated Articles:** Leviticus 17:11 (ID: `20167@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Acts 15:29 (id: 9373)

Farewell: The letter to the churches includes this familiar Hellenistic greeting at the end of a letter, found only here in the New Testament. Paul often ended his letters on a more theological note (e.g., [1 Cor 16:23–24](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:23-1Cor16:24); [2 Cor 13:13](https://ref.ly/2Cor13:13); see [Eph 6:23–24](https://ref.ly/Eph6:23-Eph6:24); [Phil 4:23](https://ref.ly/Phil4:23); [Col 4:18](https://ref.ly/Col4:18); [1 Thes 5:23–28](https://ref.ly/1Thess5:23-1Thess5:28); [1 Tim 6:21](https://ref.ly/1Tim6:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:29

## Acts 15:30–31 (id: 9374)

The believers at Antioch received the decision with great joy that conversion to Judaism and keeping all the requirements of the law of Moses was not required of them. The dispute had been resolved, so the work of teaching and preaching in Antioch could continue unimpeded.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:30-31

## Acts 15:36–41 (id: 9375)

The discordant separation of Paul and Barnabas is indicated by a very strong word in Greek (their disagreement was so sharp). It was an honest disagreement between two godly men about whether John Mark (Barnabas’s cousin, [Col 4:10](https://ref.ly/Col4:10)) should be given a second chance to accompany them on a mission journey after he had deserted them in Pamphylia (see [Acts 13:13](https://ref.ly/Acts13:13)). The rift between Paul and John Mark was eventually healed ([2 Tim 4:11](https://ref.ly/2Tim4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 15:36-41

Following his disagreement with Barnabas over John Mark, Paul chose Silas as his partner for a second missionary journey through Syria and Cilicia. The two men traveled overland to visit Derbe and Lystra, explaining the decision of the Jerusalem council and strengthening believers in their faith.

## Acts 16:3 (id: 9397)

Paul had Timothy circumcised to enhance Timothy’s acceptance and effectiveness as a Jew in Jewish circles (contrast Titus, who was a full Gentile, [Gal 2:3](https://ref.ly/Gal2:3)). Elsewhere Paul clarified that it makes no difference to God whether one is circumcised or not circumcised ([Rom 2:25–29](https://ref.ly/Rom2:25-Rom2:29); [Gal 5:6](https://ref.ly/Gal5:6); [6:15](https://ref.ly/Gal6:15)). Paul was prepared to use any legitimate means to communicate the Good News ([1 Cor 9:20–21](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:20-1Cor9:21)) to various audiences.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`)

## Acts 16:4–5 (id: 9398)

Paul and Silas faithfully communicated the decisions of the Jerusalem council. The wisdom of the decision was indicated as the churches were strengthened in their faith and grew larger every day (cp. [2:41](https://ref.ly/Acts2:41); [4:4](https://ref.ly/Acts4:4); [5:14](https://ref.ly/Acts5:14); [6:1](https://ref.ly/Acts6:1); [9:31](https://ref.ly/Acts9:31); [21:20](https://ref.ly/Acts21:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Acts 16:6–10 (id: 9400)

God directed the missionaries’ travels: The Holy Spirit had prevented them from proceeding westward into the province of Asia, the Spirit of Jesus did not allow them to go north to Bithynia*,* and then Paul had a vision calling them to go northwest over the Aegean Sea to Macedonia. God’s Spirit guided his servants in Acts in a variety of ways, including divine visions (see also [9:10](https://ref.ly/Acts9:10); [10:9–16](https://ref.ly/Acts10:9-Acts10:16); [22:18](https://ref.ly/Acts22:18)), direct intuition (cp. [8:29](https://ref.ly/Acts8:29), [39](https://ref.ly/Acts8:39); [10:19](https://ref.ly/Acts10:19); [20:22](https://ref.ly/Acts20:22)), counsel with other believers (cp. [15:29](https://ref.ly/Acts15:29)), guidance through prayer ([13:2](https://ref.ly/Acts13:2), [4](https://ref.ly/Acts13:4)), insight through Scripture ([28:25–27](https://ref.ly/Acts28:25-Acts28:27)), and prophecy ([11:28](https://ref.ly/Acts11:28)). Guidance by the indwelling Holy Spirit helped them to accomplish their mission to be Christ’s witnesses ([1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); see also [4:8](https://ref.ly/Acts4:8), [31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:31); [11:24](https://ref.ly/Acts11:24); [13:9](https://ref.ly/Acts13:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:6-10

## Acts 16:8 (id: 9401)

Troas, a major port on the Aegean Sea, was one of the more significant cities in the Roman Empire. Paul’s strategy was often to visit principal places such as Troas, Athens, Corinth, and Ephesus. When the Christian faith had been planted in major centers, local Christian workers could carry it into outlying areas (e.g., Epaphras took the message from Ephesus to Colosse, [Col 1:7](https://ref.ly/Col1:7); [4:12–13](https://ref.ly/Col4:12-Col4:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:8

## Acts 16:10 (id: 9381)

we: The most natural interpretation of the shift from “they” to “we” is that Luke accompanied Paul from Troas to Philippi ([16:10–17](https://ref.ly/Acts16:10-Acts16:17)). Later Luke joined Paul again at Philippi and sailed with him to Troas, then to Miletus ([20:5–15](https://ref.ly/Acts20:5-Acts20:15)), then from Miletus to Jerusalem ([21:1–18](https://ref.ly/Acts21:1-Acts21:18)). After Paul’s two\-year imprisonment in Caesarea, Luke traveled with him to Rome ([27:1–28:16](https://ref.ly/Acts27:1-Acts28:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Macedonia (ID: `place:Macedonia`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)

## Acts 16:11 (id: 9382)

Samothrace is a small mountainous island lying west\-northwest of the Hellespont about twenty miles (32 km) from the coast of Thrace. • Neapolis (modern Kavala) was the seaport for Philippi and the eastern terminus of the famous *Via Egnatia* (the Egnatian Way), which ran from Rome to Asia.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:11

At Philippi, about 17 kilometers (10 miles) from the coast of Neapolis, Paul began his mission work in Europe.

## Acts 16:13 (id: 9385)

The Jewish community at Philippi was too small to have a synagogue, which required ten adult males. Instead, Jews met for prayer in an open space by the Gangites (now called Angista) River that provided privacy, quiet, and water for Jewish purification rites.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Acts 16:13–36 (id: 9384)

Luke gives his readers cameos of three lives touched by the Good News in Philippi: a wealthy woman ([16:14–15](https://ref.ly/Acts16:14-Acts16:15)), an exploited slave girl ([16:16–21](https://ref.ly/Acts16:16-Acts16:21)), and a middle\-class officer ([16:23–36](https://ref.ly/Acts16:23-Acts16:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:13-36

## Acts 16:14–15 (id: 9386)

The first person changed by Christ in Philippi was Lydia*,* a successful businesswoman from Thyatira (modern Akhisar), a city of western Asia Minor famous for its woolen fabrics, weavers, and linens. Lydia responded to the message and she and her household were baptized (cp. [16:32–33](https://ref.ly/Acts16:32-Acts16:33)). • One of Lydia’s first acts as a true believer in the Lord was to extend hospitality to the visiting missionaries. Hospitality is an important Christian virtue ([Matt 25:31–46](https://ref.ly/Matt25:31-Matt25:46); [Rom 12:13](https://ref.ly/Rom12:13); [16:23](https://ref.ly/Rom16:23); [1 Tim 3:2](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:2); [Titus 1:8](https://ref.ly/Titus1:8); [Heb 13:2](https://ref.ly/Heb13:2); [1 Pet 4:9](https://ref.ly/1Pet4:9); [3 Jn 1:5–8](https://ref.ly/3John1:5-3John1:8); cp. [Gen 18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1-Gen18:8); [19:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1-Gen19:3); [24:23–33](https://ref.ly/Gen24:23-Gen24:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Thyatira (ID: `place:Thyatira`); Lydia (ID: `person:Lydia`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Faithfulness (ID: `keyterm:Faithfulness`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Acts 16:16–18 (id: 9387)

The second portrait of a changed life in Philippi is of a slave girl who had a spirit that enabled her to tell the future. • Even though the demon within her was stating the truth, Paul, like Jesus, did not permit it to proclaim the Christian message (cp. [Mark 1:25](https://ref.ly/Mark1:25), [34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34); [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); [Luke 4:35](https://ref.ly/Luke4:35), [41](https://ref.ly/Luke4:41)). The Lord had commanded that the gospel be proclaimed by his disciples, not by opponents ([Acts 1:8](https://ref.ly/Acts1:8); [9:15](https://ref.ly/Acts9:15); [26:15–18](https://ref.ly/Acts26:15-Acts26:18); [Matt 28:18–20](https://ref.ly/Matt28:18-Matt28:20); [Luke 24:46–49](https://ref.ly/Luke24:46-Luke24:49); [John 20:21](https://ref.ly/John20:21); see[Acts 22:15](https://ref.ly/Acts22:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Acts 16:19–21 (id: 9388)

As in the ministry of Jesus, sometimes the Good News threatened established commercial interests (see also [19:25–27](https://ref.ly/Acts19:25-Acts19:27); [Mark 5:1–20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1-Mark5:20)). The slave girl’s exploiters viciously attacked the missionaries and dragged them before the authorities as criminals, blamed them for fomenting a disturbance, resorted to racial bias (these Jews), and appealed to the Philippians’ pride (us Romans*;* the Philippians prided themselves on being Roman citizens of a Roman colony). • customs that are illegal: By law, Jews were not permitted to make converts of Romans.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:19-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Hope (ID: `keyterm:Hope`)

## Acts 16:22–24 (id: 9389)

All reasonable security measures were taken to ensure that Paul and Silas didn’t escape after they had been stripped and beaten with wooden rods (see [22:24–26](https://ref.ly/Acts22:24-Acts22:26); [2 Cor 6:5](https://ref.ly/2Cor6:5); [11:23–25](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:23-2Cor11:25)). As at Christ’s tomb ([Matt 27:65](https://ref.ly/Matt27:65)), however, human effort did not prevent divine intervention.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:22-24

## Acts 16:27 (id: 9393)

The Roman jailer knew he could lose his life if the prisoners he guarded escaped. This was a common practice ([12:19](https://ref.ly/Acts12:19); [27:42](https://ref.ly/Acts27:42); compare *Code of Justinian* 9\.4\.4\).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); Door (ID: `realia:Door`)

## Acts 16:27–36 (id: 9392)

Luke’s third portrait in Philippi is of the Philippian jailer who, shaken by what had happened, responded in faith when challenged to accept the Lord Jesus and be saved. He was baptized with his household and rejoiced in his newfound faith as he reached out with hospitality to the preachers.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:27-36

## Acts 16:29–30 (id: 9394)

Paul and Silas had impressed the jailer with their cheerful faith ([16:25](https://ref.ly/Acts16:25)), their composure in crisis, and their concern for his well\-being ([16:28](https://ref.ly/Acts16:28)). Whatever the jailer’s previous understanding of Paul’s message, God touched his heart, and he cried out for divine help.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Torch (ID: `realia:Torch`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Silas (ID: `person:Silas`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Acts 16:31–34 (id: 9395)

Paul and Silas directed the jailer to a faith in Christ that brings blessing both to him and to his family. The whole household received Christian instruction, and their response was expressed in baptism and the offering of hospitality to Paul and Silas.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:31-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Acts 16:37–39 (id: 9396)

Paul and Silas were both Roman citizens*,* so the beating and imprisonment ([16:22–24](https://ref.ly/Acts16:22-Acts16:24)) had been illegal. After the city officials learned of this, they were justifiably alarmed because they had committed a crime against Rome. • Let them come themselves to release us: Paul made use of his rights as a Roman citizen in order to clear his name and ensure that no disrepute would attach to the Christian message or those who accepted it. Luke intended to show that Christianity should enjoy the same status as Judaism in the Roman Empire as a permitted religion—the Christian faith was compatible with the life of a Roman citizen. Accordingly, it was important to note that Paul made use of his rights as a Roman citizen (cp. [22:25–27](https://ref.ly/Acts22:25-Acts22:27)) and was ready to use the privileges of citizenship to advance the cause of Christ in a hostile world.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 16:37-39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rome (ID: `place:Rome`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Acts 17:1–3 (id: 9403)

Amphipolis was a Roman military post located on the Egnatian Way in the northeastern part of Macedonia. • Apollonia*,* named after the Greek god Apollo, was also situated on the Egnatian Way. • Thessalonica offered a starting point for ministry because it had a Jewish synagogue (see study note on 9:2). Paul was able to preach for three consecutive Sabbaths*,* explaining the Scriptures and showing their fulfillment in Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:1-3

## Acts 17:10–12 (id: 9405)

In light of the strong opposition in Thessalonica, the believers sent Paul and Silas to Berea*,* located about fifty miles (80\.5 km) west of Thessalonica. There the missionaries had a better reception than in Thessalonica. Many Jews came to faith, as well as many of the prominent Greek women and men. The Bereans were exemplary in their attitude, for they were open\-minded and eager to learn, good listeners, diligent Bible students, and thoughtful people. Their resulting faith had a strong foundation.

## Acts 17:16–17 (id: 9407)

Athens, like Alexandria and Tarsus, was proud of its learning and love of ideas. People there spent time studying and discussing different philosophies (ways of thinking about life and the world) that were popular at that time.

## Acts 17:16–34 (id: 9408)

In this chapter, we see Paul presented as a model witness for Christ, engaging the thinkers of his day and challenging them with the Christian message. Paul quoted writers his audience would be familiar with and showed the relevance of the gospel by dialoguing with them, critiquing their assumptions, and offering Jesus as a constructive alternative (see [Col 1:28](https://ref.ly/Col1:28)). Paul reminded these proud intellectuals that there is a living God to whom all human beings are answerable; that they will be judged by him through Jesus, whom God raised from the dead; and that they should therefore repent and put their faith in Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:16-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Areopagus (ID: `place:Areopagus`); Athens (ID: `place:Athens`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Epicureans (ID: `group:Epicurean`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Areopagites (ID: `group:Areopagus`); Athenians (ID: `group:Athens`); Stoics (ID: `group:Stoic`); Damaris (ID: `person:Damaris`); Dionysius (ID: `person:Dionysius`); Divine (ID: `keyterm:Divine`); Object of Worship (ID: `keyterm:ObjectOfWorship`); Bring to the Attention Of (ID: `keyterm:BringToTheAttentionOf`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Proof (ID: `keyterm:Proof`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Obligated (ID: `keyterm:Ought`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`)

## Acts 17:18 (id: 9409)

* *Epicurean philosophers* followed Epicureanism, a Greek school of thought founded by Epicurus, who lived from 341 to 270 BC. Epicureans believed that the main goal of life was happiness. They did not define pleasure as physical excess, as some critics said. Instead, they valued peace of mind and freedom from fear. Many people in their time called them atheists because they did not believe the gods controlled human life. They also believed that death was simply the end of a person’s existence. For safety and peace, they formed organized communities and lived apart from wider society.
* *Stoic philosophers* followed Stoicism, founded by Zeno of Citium, who lived from 335 to 263 BC. Stoicism became one of the most influential philosophies in the Greco\-Roman world. Stoics believed that the universe was filled with Reason, which they sometimes called God or Providence. They believed this divine Reason could also be seen in human reason. As people grew in understanding, they could move from ignorance, which leads to vice, to true knowledge, which leads to virtue.

    Stoics made detailed lists of virtues and vices. They also wrote household codes, which were instructions for family life. In some ways, Paul’s teaching sounds similar, especially in his household codes and lists of virtues and vices ([Galatians 5:19–23](https://ref.ly/Gal5:19-Gal5:23); [Ephesians 5:22–33](https://ref.ly/Eph5:22-Eph5:33); [Colossians 3:18–4:1](https://ref.ly/Col3:18-Col4:1); [1 Timothy 3:1–13](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:1-1Tim3:13); [5:1–6:1](https://ref.ly/1Tim5:1-1Tim5:6)). However, Paul's message about the life, death, and resurrection of Jesus Christ was unfamiliar to these Greek philosophers.

* Their insulting use of the word *babbler* for Paul also shows that they looked down on him.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Stoics (ID: `group:Stoic`); Epicureans (ID: `group:Epicurean`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)

## Acts 17:22–31 (id: 9410)

* Paul’s sermon in Athens shows how well he could adapt his preaching of the good news ([Romans 11:14](https://ref.ly/Rom11:14); [1 Corinthians 9:19–23](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:19-1Cor9:23); [10:33](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:33); compare [Acts 16:3](https://ref.ly/Acts16:3); [17:2–3](https://ref.ly/Acts17:2-Acts17:3); [21:20–26](https://ref.ly/Acts21:20-Acts21:26)). His Greek audience did not know the Scriptures or believe in one God like the Jews. However, they had a strong intellectual background. Paul connected with them using an Athenian inscription on an altar *TO AN UNKNOWN GOD*. He then explained God’s nature as the Creator ([17:22–29](https://ref.ly/Acts17:22-Acts17:29)), followed by God’s purpose as the Redeemer ([17:30–31](https://ref.ly/Acts17:30-Acts17:31)).
* As he did in his letters (see [1 Corinthians 15:33](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:33); [Titus 1:12](https://ref.ly/Titus1:12)), Paul also used lines from Greek poets. His sermon has several ideas similar to the *Hymn to Zeus* by Cleanthes, who lived around 315 to 240 BC. Paul likely quoted Epimenides, who wrote, "In him we live and move and exist" (Epimenides, *Cretica,* about 600 BC). Paul also quoted Aratus, a Stoic (a follower of Stoicism, a Greek philosophy that valued reason and virtue) from Cilicia, who wrote, "We are his offspring" (Aratus, *Phaenomena* 5\).

    By using these familiar sayings, Paul connected with his audience. Then he moved to his main message. He taught that God is one and not like idols made by humans ([Acts 17:29](https://ref.ly/Acts17:29)). He also warned that God will judge the world through Jesus Christ, whom he raised from the dead.

    Even though Paul spoke in ways that related to Greek culture and thought, his message clearly pointed to Christ. He challenged an educated but idol\-worshiping audience to respond to the truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:22-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Areopagus (ID: `place:Areopagus`); Decided (ID: `keyterm:Decided`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Divine (ID: `keyterm:Divine`); Object of Worship (ID: `keyterm:ObjectOfWorship`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Athens (ID: `place:Athens`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Proof (ID: `keyterm:Proof`); Obligated (ID: `keyterm:Ought`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`)

## Acts 17:30 (id: 9411)

The idea of people’s ignorance is carefully discussed both in the Old Testament law ([Leviticus 4:2](https://ref.ly/Lev4:2), [22](https://ref.ly/Lev4:22), [27](https://ref.ly/Lev4:27); [5:15](https://ref.ly/Lev5:15), [17](https://ref.ly/Lev5:17); [Numbers 15:25](https://ref.ly/Num15:25), [27](https://ref.ly/Num15:27)). It is also present in the New Testament ([Ephesians 4:18](https://ref.ly/Eph4:18); [1 Peter 1:14](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:14); [2:15](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:15); [2 Peter 3:5](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:5), [8](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:8)). Paul was especially eager to correct ignorance (see [Romans 10:13–15](https://ref.ly/Rom10:13-Rom10:15); [11:25](https://ref.ly/Rom11:25); [1 Corinthians 10:1](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:1); [12:1](https://ref.ly/1Cor12:1); [2 Corinthians 1:8](https://ref.ly/2Cor1:8); [1 Thessalonians 4:13](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:13)). The message of the gospel overcomes ignorance and calls all people to repent "and be baptized … and receive the gift of the Holy Spirit" (see [Acts 2:38](https://ref.ly/Acts2:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`)

## Acts 17:32 (id: 9412)

The Athenians listened carefully to Paul until he spoke about the resurrection of the dead ([17:31](https://ref.ly/Acts17:31)). At that point, some began to laugh and mock him. To Greek listeners, the idea of resurrection seemed foolish (see [1 Corinthians 15:12–19](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:12-1Cor15:19)). In a similar way, the Jews in Jerusalem listened to Paul until he spoke about God accepting the Gentiles ([Acts 22:22](https://ref.ly/Acts22:22)). Then they also rejected his message.

These examples show how the message of the good news can offend people because of their prejudices (strong opinions that keep them from accepting the truth; see [1 Corinthians 1:20–25](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:20-1Cor1:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`)

## Acts 17:34 (id: 9413)

* *some joined him and believed*: Some people joined Paul and became believers. His sermon did not only bring rejection; it also led to faith.

    Two important people who believed are named: Dionysius and Damaris. Luke often places a man and a woman side by side in his writing to show that the message of the good news is for all people. By pairing men and women, he shows that both are equally important in God’s work (for example, [5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts5:1-Acts5:11); [17:4](https://ref.ly/Acts17:4), [12](https://ref.ly/Acts17:12); [Luke 14:1–6](https://ref.ly/Luke14:1-Luke14:6); [15:3–10](https://ref.ly/Luke15:3-Luke15:10)).

* Dionysius was a member of the council of the Areopagus, the highest governing body of Athens.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 17:34

## Acts 18:1 (id: 9423)

In Roman times, political power in Achaia was centered in Corinth, an important city\-state and a major center for trade. Corinth became known for widespread prostitution, sexual immorality (wrong sexual behavior), and drunkenness (see 1 Corinthians Book Introduction, “Setting”). 

Paul spent a great deal of time and effort building a Christian community there (see study note on Acts 16:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:1

Paul spent 18 productive months preaching and teaching in Corinth. He started in the synagogue and then moved next door to the house of Titius Justus ([18:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts18:1-Acts18:11)). Then Paul won an important case in court against his opponents ([18:12–17](https://ref.ly/Acts18:12-Acts18:17)).

## Acts 18:2–3 (id: 9424)

* The Roman historian Suetonius mentions that the emperor Claudius Caesar ordered all Jews to leave Rome around AD 49 (*Life of Claudius* 25\).
* Aquila and Priscilla were a remarkable husband and wife team in the early church. They worked as tentmakers, as did Paul. Paul probably lived and worked with Aquila and Priscilla during his time in Corinth ([Acts 18:11](https://ref.ly/Acts18:11); see [Romans 16:3–4](https://ref.ly/Rom16:3-Rom16:4); [1 Corinthians 16:19](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:19)).
* Paul had probably learned tentmaking when he was young. It was a Jewish custom to teach sons a manual trade, even if they planned to become rabbis or other kinds of professionals.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:2-3

* Paul followed his usual practice of preaching to the Jews first. After encountering rejection and opposition, he then reached out to the Gentiles ([13:42–49](https://ref.ly/Acts13:42-Acts13:49); see [3:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts3:25-Acts3:26); [26:20](https://ref.ly/Acts26:20); [Romans 1:16](https://ref.ly/Rom1:16); [2:10](https://ref.ly/Rom2:10); [3:29](https://ref.ly/Rom3:29), [30](https://ref.ly/Rom3:30); [4:9–12](https://ref.ly/Rom4:9-Rom4:12)).
* Paul probably wrote his letters to the Christians in Thessalonica after Silas and Timothy returned from Macedonia with a report on the situation there (see [1 Thessalonians 3:6](https://ref.ly/1Thess3:6); 2 Thessalonians Book Introduction, “Setting”).

## Acts 18:7 (id: 9430)

Titius Justus (“Titus the Just”) was a Gentile who worshiped God. People like him are often called “God\-fearers” (non\-Jews who worship the God of Israel; see study note on 10:2). The name *Titus* was a common Roman name, so the second name or title *Justus* helps to tell him apart from the better\-known Titus, who worked with Paul ("Titus" in [2 Corinthians 2:13](https://ref.ly/2Cor2:13); [7:6](https://ref.ly/2Cor7:6), [13](https://ref.ly/2Cor7:13); [8:6](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:6), [16](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:16), [23](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:23)).

Paul encountered strong opposition in Corinth and feared another attack ([18:6](https://ref.ly/Acts18:6)). The divine message comforted him (see [23:11](https://ref.ly/Acts23:11); [Psalm 34:4](https://ref.ly/Ps34:4), [7](https://ref.ly/Ps34:7), [19](https://ref.ly/Ps34:19); [Matthew 28:20](https://ref.ly/Matt28:20)). It encouraged him to continue his public ministry and promised God's protection (see [Psalm 91:11](https://ref.ly/Ps91:11); [2 Timothy 4:17](https://ref.ly/2Tim4:17)).

## Acts 18:12–13 (id: 9418)

The governor (or proconsul) of Achaia, Junius Gallio, was the older brother of the Roman philosopher Seneca. Seneca later served as a tutor to Emperor Nero.

An ancient inscription shows that Gallio was governor around AD 51–52\. This helps scholars date Paul’s visit to Corinth to about the same time.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:12-13

Gallio’s ruling indicated that the charges against Paul were unjustified. The Roman government had nothing to fear from acknowledging Christianity as a legal religion.

The Greeks probably beat Sosthenes to show their anger towards the Jews. It is also possible that the Jews attacked their own synagogue leader because he failed to defend them. Sosthenes might be the same person Paul later calls "our brother" in [1 Corinthians 1:1](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:1), but this is uncertain since the name was common.

## Acts 18:18 (id: 9421)

Cenchrea was a seaport on the Aegean Sea, about eight kilometers (five miles) east of Corinth. Here, Paul had his head shaved following Jewish custom to mark the completion of a temporary Nazirite vow (compare [21:23–24](https://ref.ly/Acts21:23-Acts21:24); and [Numbers 6:1–21](https://ref.ly/Num6:1-Num6:21); [Judges 13:4–7](https://ref.ly/Judg13:4-Judg13:7); [16:1](https://ref.ly/Judg16:1); [Amos 2:11–12](https://ref.ly/Amos2:11-Amos2:12); [Luke 1:15](https://ref.ly/Luke1:15)).

## Acts 18:19–23 (id: 9422)

Paul made a quick stop at Ephesus*,* the most important city in the Roman province of Asia. There he left the others behind*,* including Priscilla and Aquila (see [18:26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26)). 

He sailed to Judea, landing at Caesarea*,* the headquarters of the Roman forces of occupation. After a visit to the church at Jerusalem*,* Paul returned to Antioch*,* the church that had originally commissioned him. This marked the end of his second missionary journey.

In Antioch, he spent valuable time reporting what God had done through him and those who ministered with him. He shared the excitement and challenges of their work with the home church. 

After some time, Paul began his third mission journey. He traveled by land through Galatia and Phrygia, again visiting Christians he had led to faith in Christ on his previous trips. It was important to him that these new believers did not lose their way or abandon their faith (see [Ephesians 6:10–20](https://ref.ly/Eph6:10-Eph6:20); [1 Timothy 1:18–20](https://ref.ly/1Tim1:18-1Tim1:20); [2 Timothy 1:15](https://ref.ly/2Tim1:15); [4:10](https://ref.ly/2Tim4:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:19-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ephesus (ID: `place:Ephesus`); Phrygia (ID: `place:Phrygia`); Galatia (ID: `place:Galatia`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Caesarea (ID: `place:Caesarea`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Antioch (ID: `place:Antioch`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Acts 18:23–19.41 (id: 9426)

This section describes Paul's third mission journey, which happened around AD 53 to 57\. Paul visited Galatia and Phrygia again to build stronger relationships with the disciples ([18:23](https://ref.ly/Acts18:23)). He then traveled to Ephesus, where he stayed for more than two years ([19:1,](https://ref.ly/Acts19:1) [8–10](https://ref.ly/Acts19:8-Acts19:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:23-19:41

## Acts 18:24–26 (id: 9428)

Alexandria was the second\-largest city in the Roman Empire. It was known for its strong tradition of public speaking and for the writings of Philo, a Jewish philosopher.

Apollos was a skilled speaker and knew the Scriptures (the Old Testament) very well. However, his understanding of Jesus and the Holy Spirit was incomplete. He did not yet understand that believers can experience the Holy Spirit’s power in their lives now.

Priscilla and Aquila kindly took him aside and explained the way of God more accurately.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 18:24-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Apollos (ID: `person:Apollos`); Alexandria (ID: `place:Alexandria`); Alexandrians (ID: `group:Alexandria`); Aquila (ID: `person:Aquila`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Written (ID: `keyterm:Scripture`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Ephesus (ID: `place:Ephesus`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); John (ID: `person:John`); Priscilla (ID: `person:Priscilla`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Acts 19:1–7 (id: 9432)

* Paul traveled to Ephesus after Apollos had left. Later, Apollos returned to Ephesus while Paul was still there ([1 Corinthians 16:12](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:12)). The two men had different personalities, gifts, and roles, but God worked through both of them.
* Some Christians in Ephesus did not fully understand the Christian faith ([Acts 18:26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:26)). They did not receive the Holy Spirit when they believed the good news about Jesus. They had accepted John's baptism, which called for repentance from sin, but had not received Christian baptism, which included the gift of the Holy Spirit. When Paul had instructed them further, they were able to receive Christian baptism and the Holy Spirit ([19:5,](https://ref.ly/Acts19:5) [6](https://ref.ly/Acts19:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); John (ID: `person:John`); Ephesus (ID: `place:Ephesus`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Corinth (ID: `place:Corinth`); Apollos (ID: `person:Apollos`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`)

## Acts 19:6 (id: 9448)

When Paul laid his hands on them, they received the Holy Spirit. They spoke in other tongues (languages given by the Spirit) and prophesied (spoke messages from God). In this way, they experienced the same filling with God’s power and presence that the disciples received on the day of Pentecost ([2:4](https://ref.ly/Acts2:4), [11](https://ref.ly/Acts2:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`)

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:10

## Acts 19:11–12 (id: 9434)

* Paul, like other Christian messengers who preached the good news, performed unusual miracles ([3:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts3:1-Acts3:11); [4:22](https://ref.ly/Acts4:22); [5:12–16](https://ref.ly/Acts5:12-Acts5:16); [6:8](https://ref.ly/Acts6:8); [8:6–7](https://ref.ly/Acts8:6-Acts8:7), [13](https://ref.ly/Acts8:13); [9:33–42](https://ref.ly/Acts9:33-Acts9:42)).
* *evil spirits left them*: Evil spirits were driven out, just as Jesus and Peter had done before (compare [5:16](https://ref.ly/Acts5:16); [Mark 1:21–34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:34); [Luke 4:31–37](https://ref.ly/Luke4:31-Luke4:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:11-12

## Acts 19:13–16 (id: 9435)

Paul’s ministry was effective, but the traveling Jewish exorcists (people who try to drive out evil spirits) had no real power. They used the name of Jesus as if it were a magic word. However, they did not have a personal relationship with him or the indwelling Holy Spirit (God’s Spirit living in believers).

This contrast shows the difference between true faith and empty practice (compare [Matthew 12:27](https://ref.ly/Matt12:27); [Mark 9:38–39](https://ref.ly/Mark9:38-Mark9:39); [Luke 9:49–50](https://ref.ly/Luke9:49-Luke9:50); [11:19](https://ref.ly/Luke11:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:13-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Sceva (ID: `person:Sceva`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Acts 19:17–19 (id: 9436)

The people of Ephesus, both Jews and Greeks, noticed the difference between Paul and the Jewish exorcists. This realization caused them to honor and respect the name of the Lord Jesus. Many people confessed and gave up their sinful practices, including magic.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:17-19

## Acts 19:20 (id: 9437)

Luke’s summary of the success of the gospel in Ephesus is short but powerful (compare [2:43–47](https://ref.ly/Acts2:43-Acts2:47); [5:14](https://ref.ly/Acts5:14); [6:7](https://ref.ly/Acts6:7); [9:31](https://ref.ly/Acts9:31); [12:24](https://ref.ly/Acts12:24); [16:5](https://ref.ly/Acts16:5); and [Romans 1:16](https://ref.ly/Rom1:16)). When the Christian faith overcame problems inside the church, such as division, idolatry (worship of false gods), and pagan practices (religious customs not centered on the true God), it spread widely and grew quickly.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Acts 19:21–22 (id: 9438)

* *Paul resolved in the Spirit*:He was probably troubled by what he heard about Corinth (see 1 Corinthians Book Introduction). Instead of going right away, he sent Timothy. Timothy carried a letter, 1 Corinthians, from Paul to the Corinthians ([1 Corinthians 4:17](https://ref.ly/1Cor4:17); [16:10–11](https://ref.ly/1Cor16:10-1Cor16:11)). Paul later traveled to Macedonia and Achaia ([Acts 20:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts20:1-Acts20:3)).
* *“I must see Rome as well”*: Paul, perhaps inspired by his successful strategy, wanted to share the gospel in the world's most important city (see study note on 16:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:21-22
* **Associated Articles:** Acts 16:8 (ID: `9401@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Acts 19:23 (id: 9440)

*the Way*: See study note on 9:2.

## Acts 19:23–41 (id: 9439)

The main god worshiped in Ephesus was the Greek goddess Artemis. People believed she was born there, so the city of Ephesus was known as the guardian of her temple.

Each year, the people held large festivals in her honor. These included sports, music, and theater. During these events, people would shout, “Great is Artemis of the Ephesians!”

The temple of Artemis in Ephesus was one of the seven wonders of the ancient world.

As more people became Christians, the worship of Artemis began to decline. This also affected the local economy, since many people earned money from her temple. However, it became clear that Paul and his coworkers had not committed any crime.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:23-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Artemis (ID: `deity:Artemis`); Ephesus (ID: `place:Ephesus`); Ephesians (ID: `group:Ephesus`); Paul (ID: `person:Paul`); Demetrius (ID: `person:Demetrius`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Asia (ID: `place:Asia`); Theater (ID: `realia:Theater`); Alexander (ID: `person:Alexander.3`); Gaius (ID: `person:Gaius`); Aristarchus (ID: `person:Aristarchus`); Persuade (ID: `keyterm:Persuade`); Public Official (ID: `person:GenericOfficial`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Macedonia (ID: `place:Macedonia`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Replica Temple (ID: `realia:ReplicaTemple`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

Archaeologists have uncovered the amphitheater where the whole city gathered. It could hold about 24,000 people.

## Acts 19:31 (id: 9445)

*officials of the province of Asia*: These officials, called *Asiarchs* ("rulers of Asia"), were the appointed leaders in the Roman province of Asia. They served as financial supporters of the city and often supported the worship of the emperor. Some of these high\-ranking officials were friends of Paul. Luke shows that the Christian faith also attracted people of high social status.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 19:31

The tradition said that the image (statue) of Artemis had fallen from heaven. This may mean that it was made from a meteor (a rock that falls to earth from space).

## Acts 20:1–2 (id: 9463)

Paul traveled to Macedonia, where he encouraged Christians in towns like Thessalonica, Philippi, and Berea. He also continued collecting donations for needy Christians in Jerusalem (see [Romans 15:25–28](https://ref.ly/Rom15:25-Rom15:28); [2 Corinthians 8:1–9:15](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:1-2Cor9:15)). 

Titus met Paul in Macedonia with news from Corinth, prompting Paul to write 2 Corinthians and send Titus back with the letter ([2 Corinthians 7:5–7](https://ref.ly/2Cor7:5-2Cor7:7); [8:6](https://ref.ly/2Cor8:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 20:1-2

## Acts 20:7–12 (id: 9477)

* On the first day of the week, the early church remembered the resurrection of Jesus (see [Mark 16:9](https://ref.ly/Mark16:9); compare [John 20:19](https://ref.ly/John20:19); [Revelation 1:10](https://ref.ly/Rev1:10)).
* This was Paul’s last visit to Troas. The believers met to share the Lord’s Supper (a meal that remembers Jesus’s death). This likely included both the bread and cup (communion) and a shared meal (compare [Acts 2:42](https://ref.ly/Acts2:42), [46](https://ref.ly/Acts2:46); [Jude 1:12](https://ref.ly/Jude1:12)).
* One notable part of this gathering was the quiet miracle involving Eutychus. He was a young man who fell asleep while sitting in a window. He fell three stories and died. Paul went down, bent over him, and took him in his arms. Then Eutychus was restored to life (God brought him back to life through Paul; compare [Acts 9:36–41](https://ref.ly/Acts9:36-Acts9:41)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 20:7-12

## Acts 20:17 (id: 9466)

Miletus was a major sea port on the western coast of Asia Minor at the opening of the Meander River. Paul met briefly with the elders of the church of Ephesus there on his way to Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 20:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Miletus (ID: `place:Miletus`); Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Ephesus (ID: `place:Ephesus`)

## Acts 20:18–38 (id: 9467)

Paul’s message to the elders (leaders) of the church in Ephesus describes his life and work among them. He also calls these leaders to show the same kind of commitment as they continue the ministry.

In this message, Paul points to his own life as an example. He shows his honesty and care for people ([20:18–21](https://ref.ly/Acts20:18-Acts20:21), [26](https://ref.ly/Acts20:26), [31](https://ref.ly/Acts20:31)). He also speaks about what will happen in the future ([20:22–23](https://ref.ly/Acts20:22-Acts20:23), [25](https://ref.ly/Acts20:25), [29–30](https://ref.ly/Acts20:29-Acts20:30)). He warns them about false teachers (people who teach wrong ideas about God; [20:29–30](https://ref.ly/Acts20:29-Acts20:30)). And he urges the elders to stay alert and faithful ([20:28](https://ref.ly/Acts20:28), [31](https://ref.ly/Acts20:31)). 

Through his example, Paul shows what it means to lead as a servant, with sacrifice and careful attention.

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 20:18-38

## Acts 20:28 (id: 9471)

* Paul describes the church as God’s people and God’s flock (a group cared for by a shepherd; compare [1 Peter 2:25](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:25); [5:2](https://ref.ly/1Pet5:2), [4](https://ref.ly/1Pet5:4)). In other places, the church is described as:

    + "the body of Christ" ([1 Corinthians 12:27](https://ref.ly/1Cor12:27); [Ephesians 1:23](https://ref.ly/Eph1:23); [4:12](https://ref.ly/Eph4:12); [Colossians 1:24](https://ref.ly/Col1:24));
        + the "bride" of Christ ([2 Corinthians 11:2](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:2); [Revelation 19:7](https://ref.ly/Rev19:7); see [Ephesians 5:25–32](https://ref.ly/Eph5:25-Eph5:32);
        + "God's temple" ([1 Corinthians 3:16](https://ref.ly/1Cor3:16); [2 Corinthians 6:16](https://ref.ly/2Cor6:16)).
        + "a chosen people, a royal priesthood, a holy nation, a people for God’s own possession" ([1 Peter 2:9](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:9)); and
        + "God's field, God's building" ([1 Corinthians 3:9](https://ref.ly/1Cor3:9)).

* Paul expected the leaders to care for and guide the church, like shepherds care for their flock. The Holy Spirit had appointed them to this role(see [1 Timothy 3:1–7](https://ref.ly/1Tim3:1-1Tim3:7); [Titus 1:5–7](https://ref.ly/Titus1:5-Titus1:7); [1 Peter 5:1–4](https://ref.ly/1Pet5:1-1Pet5:4); compare [Acts 6:2–4](https://ref.ly/Acts6:2-Acts6:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Acts 20:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Church (ID: `keyterm:Church`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)
